## 37 Bayonet mount (push-and-twist lock)

*quick-release & tool-free.* Push in and twist a short way so pins or lugs slide under ledges and lock; used on camera lenses, light bulbs and lids

*Not researched yet.*

## 38 Interrupted-screw lock (interrupted thread, breech lock)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A thread with gaps cut in it, so the parts push together and lock with a fraction of a turn instead of many turns

*Not researched yet.*

## 39 Quarter-turn fastener (Dzus-type)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A stud that locks a panel in place with a quarter turn, by a spiral slot riding over a spring or a cross pin

*Not researched yet.*

## 40 Quick-release pin (ball-lock pin)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A pin with small balls near its tip that lock it in a hole until you press a button on the end to let the balls retract

*Not researched yet.*

## 41 Quick-release plate (camera plate, dovetail clamp)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A plate with angled edges slides or drops into a matching clamp and is locked by a lever or knob, as on camera tripods

*Also covers:* Also covers Picatinny-style rails and camera cold shoes

*Not researched yet.*

## 42 Hang-on mounts (keyhole slot, French cleat)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A head slides into a keyhole-shaped slot, or two bevelled rails hook together, so things hang securely and lift off without tools

*Not researched yet.*

## 43 Modular grid mounting systems (pegboard, Gridfinity-style grids)

*quick-release & tool-free.* Boards or baseplates with a regular grid of holes or sockets that hooks, bins and holders snap, twist, peg or magnet into anywhere on the grid

*Also covers:* Types, not products: cover the common grid ideas (pegboard hooks, locking pegboard hooks, baseplate-and-bin grids with magnets, snap-and-thread boards)

*Not researched yet.*

## 44 Cantilever snap fit

*snap & clip fits.* A flexible arm with a hook on the end bends as parts push together, then springs back to catch a ledge; the most common snap fit (includes U- and L-shaped arms)

*Not researched yet.*

## 45 Annular snap fit

*snap & clip fits.* A round bead or groove all the way around a part snaps over a matching lip, like a pen cap or a bottle lid clicking on

*Not researched yet.*

## 46 Torsional snap fit

*snap & clip fits.* A catch on a twisting bar; press one end and the other end lifts to release, so it can be opened again and again

*Not researched yet.*

## 47 Snap pin (split-leg push-in pin, rabbit clip)

*snap & clip fits.* A pin with split, springy legs and a barb that squeezes through a hole and springs open to hold two parts together

*Not researched yet.*

## 48 Push-in plastic rivet (fir-tree clip, push rivet)

*snap & clip fits.* A ribbed or expanding plastic pin that pushes into a hole and grips, as used for car trim and panels

*Not researched yet.*

## 49 Living hinge

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* A thin, flexible strip of the same material joining two parts so they fold, like the lid of a ketchup bottle or a tic-tac box

*Not researched yet.*

## 50 Flexure hinge (notch, leaf-spring and cross-axis pivots)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* A pivot made by a thin or crossed springy section that bends instead of rubbing, giving smooth, backlash-free rotation over a small angle

*Not researched yet.*

## 51 Parallel leaf-spring flexure (linear flexure stage)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* Two or more parallel springy blades that let a platform slide a short way in a straight line with no sliding friction or play

*Not researched yet.*

## 52 Compliant gripper and force amplifier

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* One-piece bending mechanisms that multiply or redirect a force, such as printed grippers, pliers and crimpers

*Not researched yet.*

## 53 Fin-ray gripper (fin ray effect finger)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* A flexible triangular finger with cross ribs that wraps around an object when pressed, instead of pushing it away; popular for soft robot grippers

*Not researched yet.*

## 54 Self-folding print (4D printing)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* A flat print with stresses built into its layers, or a dissolvable lock holding back a spring, that folds itself into shape when heated or wetted

*Not researched yet.*

## 55 Print-in-place hinge

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* A hinge printed already assembled, with cone-shaped pins and a small gap so it moves straight off the print bed

*Not researched yet.*

## 56 Heat-set threaded insert

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* A knurled brass insert pressed into a plastic hole with a hot soldering iron, giving strong, reusable metal threads in a printed part

*Not researched yet.*

## 57 Captive nut pocket (nut trap)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* A hexagonal pocket or side slot in a printed part that holds a nut so a bolt can be tightened without a spanner

*Not researched yet.*

## 58 Printed screw thread

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* Threads modelled and printed directly into the parts, for jar lids, caps and coarse screws, with extra clearance so they turn

*Not researched yet.*

## 59 Crush ribs and spring-fit features (compliant press fit)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* Small ribs or springy tabs that squash or flex as a part is pushed in, giving a tight fit even when printer or laser sizes vary

*Not researched yet.*

## 60 Mid-print embedding (captive nuts, magnets and bearings)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* Pausing a print to drop in a nut, magnet or bearing and then printing over it, so the part is trapped inside for good

*Not researched yet.*

## 61 Heat staking (melted plastic rivet)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* A plastic post pushed through a hole and melted over with heat to form a rivet head that holds the parts together

*Not researched yet.*

## 62 Split-part joints for large prints (dovetail keys, puzzle joints, alignment pins)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* Ways to cut a model too big for the printer into pieces that slide, lock or peg back together accurately

*Not researched yet.*

## 63 Geared hinge (continuous gear hinge)

*hinges & pivots.* A hinge where two meshing gear-toothed edges roll on each other, so both leaves move together evenly; can be printed in place

*Not researched yet.*

## 64 Friction hinge (torque hinge)

*hinges & pivots.* A hinge with built-in friction that stays at any angle you leave it, like a laptop screen

*Not researched yet.*

## 65 Ball-and-socket joint (lockable ball head)

*hinges & pivots.* A ball held in a cup that can swivel in any direction, sometimes with a clamp to lock it; can be printed in place

*Not researched yet.*

## 66 Posable segmented arm (snap-together ball chain, gooseneck)

*hinges & pivots.* A chain of small ball-and-socket segments that snap together and hold any shape by friction, like a coolant hose or a flexible phone holder

*Not researched yet.*

## 67 Clevis joint (fork-and-pin joint, prong mount)

*hinges & pivots.* A fork on one part, a tongue on the other and a pin through both, giving a simple strong pivot; interleaved-prong action-camera mounts tighten it with a thumbscrew to set the angle

*Not researched yet.*

## 68 Rod end and ball link (rose joint, RC ball link)

*hinges & pivots.* A threaded end with a swivelling ball that joins a push rod to a lever, allowing some tilt and letting the rod length be adjusted, as in RC cars and planes

*Also covers:* Also covers RC pushrod ends such as Z-bends, clevises on threaded rods and set-screw pushrod connectors

*Not researched yet.*

## 69 Turntable bearing (lazy-Susan bearing, slewing ring)

*hinges & pivots.* A thin, wide ring bearing that lets a platform turn while carrying weight and tipping loads; printed versions use airsoft BBs as the balls

*Not researched yet.*

## 70 Telescoping tube twist lock (cam or collet twist lock)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* Tubes that slide inside each other and lock at any length when you twist a section, by squeezing a split collet or turning a cam

*Not researched yet.*

## 71 Telescoping tube lever lock (flip lock)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* A clamp collar with a flip lever that squeezes an outer tube onto an inner one, as on tripod legs and trekking poles

*Not researched yet.*

## 72 Spring button lock (snap button, push-button tube lock)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* A spring-loaded button in an inner tube pops out through holes in the outer tube to lock set lengths; press it to slide

*Not researched yet.*

## 73 Dovetail slide with gib strip (dovetail ways)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* A carriage with angled edges that slides along a matching angled track, with a tapered strip (gib) that is adjusted to take out any play

*Not researched yet.*

## 74 Threadless ball screw (rolling-ring drive)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* Three tilted bearings pressed onto a smooth spinning rod act like a nut and move along it; printable and adjustable, and it slips instead of jamming

*Not researched yet.*

## 75 Reversing screw (diamond-thread or level-wind screw)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* A shaft with two crossing grooves so a follower travels back and forth along it while the shaft keeps turning one way, as in fishing reels and filament winders

*Not researched yet.*

## 76 Multi-stage slide rigging (cascade and continuous string rigging)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* Ways of running strings or belts through nested slides so one winch extends all the stages, either together (cascade) or one after another (continuous), as in robot lifts

*Not researched yet.*

## 77 Oldham coupling

*clutches & couplings.* Three discs with tongue-and-groove slots at right angles that join two shafts which are slightly out of line, while still passing turning force

*Not researched yet.*

## 78 Jaw coupling (spider coupling)

*clutches & couplings.* Two hubs with interlocking claws and a rubbery spider between them, joining two shafts while soaking up shocks and small misalignment

*Not researched yet.*

## 79 Flexible beam coupling (helical-slot coupling)

*clutches & couplings.* A one-piece coupling with a spiral slot cut round it so it bends to join slightly misaligned shafts without backlash (common on 3D-printer leadscrews)

*Not researched yet.*

## 80 Universal joint (Hooke's joint, Cardan joint)

*clutches & couplings.* A cross-shaped piece between two forks that lets one shaft drive another at an angle

*Not researched yet.*

## 81 Dog clutch (claw clutch)

*clutches & couplings.* Two parts with interlocking teeth or lugs that slide together to lock drive and apart to disconnect it, with no slipping

*Not researched yet.*

## 82 Servo horn and spline hub

*clutches & couplings.* The toothed output shaft (spline) of a hobby servo and the arm or disc that pushes onto it and is held by a screw; how most robot parts attach to a servo

*Not researched yet.*

## 83 Series-elastic coupling (series elastic actuator)

*clutches & couplings.* A deliberate spring between a motor gearbox and its output, so the joint absorbs shocks and the spring's stretch can be measured to control force

*Not researched yet.*

## 84 Cam and follower (plate, barrel and face cams)

*motion conversion.* A shaped wheel or drum turns and pushes a follower up and down or side to side in a planned pattern

*Not researched yet.*

## 85 Scotch yoke

*motion conversion.* A pin on a turning wheel slides in a slot in a sliding bar, turning rotation into smooth back-and-forth straight-line motion

*Not researched yet.*

## 86 Slider-crank

*motion conversion.* A crank and connecting rod that turn rotation into back-and-forth sliding, or the reverse, as in an engine piston

*Not researched yet.*

## 87 Four-bar linkage (incl. parallelogram linkage)

*motion conversion.* Four bars joined in a loop by pivots, which turns one motion into another shaped motion; the basic building block of most linkages

*Also covers:* Also covers the pantograph (a parallelogram linkage that copies motion at a larger or smaller scale)

*Not researched yet.*

## 88 Rack and pinion

*motion conversion.* A gear (pinion) meshing with a toothed straight bar (rack) to turn rotation into straight-line motion or the reverse

*Not researched yet.*

## 89 Scissor linkage (lazy tongs, scissor lift)

*motion conversion.* Crossed bars pinned in the middle and at their ends that stretch out a long way from a small movement, as in scissor lifts and extending mirrors

*Not researched yet.*

## 90 Iris mechanism

*motion conversion.* Curved blades that swing in together as a ring turns, closing a round opening evenly like a camera aperture

*Not researched yet.*

## 91 Whippletree (load-equalising lever)

*motion conversion.* A floating bar pulled at its middle that shares the pull equally between its two ends, and can be stacked to share it among many, as in wiper blades and robot hands

*Not researched yet.*

## 92 Twisted-string actuator

*motion conversion.* A motor twists two or more strings together so they shorten and pull with great force, giving a cheap, light, very high-ratio linear pull

*Not researched yet.*

## 93 Planetary gear set (epicyclic gearing)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A central sun gear, planet gears around it and an outer ring gear, giving a compact, strong speed reduction with input and output on the same line

*Not researched yet.*

## 94 Compound planetary gearbox (split-ring or Wolfrom planetary)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A planetary set with two ring gears of slightly different tooth counts, giving a very high reduction in one stage; popular in 3D-printed robot actuators

*Not researched yet.*

## 95 Strain-wave gear (harmonic drive)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* An oval part flexes a thin toothed ring inside a slightly larger ring gear, giving a large, backlash-free reduction in a flat package

*Not researched yet.*

## 96 Cycloidal drive (cycloidal reducer)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A lobed disc wobbles around inside a ring of pins, turning slowly as it goes, giving a large, shock-resistant reduction

*Not researched yet.*

## 97 Differential (bevel gear differential)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A gear set that lets two output shafts turn at different speeds while sharing the drive, as between the wheels of a car

*Also covers:* Also covers the ball differential used in RC cars and limited-slip types

*Not researched yet.*

## 98 Bevel gears (incl. mitre and crown gears)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* Cone-shaped gears that pass turning force round a corner, usually at a right angle

*Not researched yet.*

## 99 Herringbone and helical gears

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* Gears with slanted teeth that mesh more smoothly and quietly than straight teeth; herringbone (V-shaped) teeth cancel sideways push and print well

*Not researched yet.*

## 100 Belt drives (timing, round and V-belt)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A loop of belt around two pulleys that passes rotation between shafts; toothed timing belts don't slip, round and V-belts can

*Not researched yet.*

## 101 Capstan cable drive (tendon drive)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A cable wrapped several turns around a small drum and anchored to a larger drum, giving a smooth, zero-backlash reduction for robot joints

*Not researched yet.*

## 102 Bowden cable (push-pull cable)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* An inner wire sliding inside a flexible outer sleeve that carries a pull or push around bends, as in bike brakes and 3D-printer extruders

*Not researched yet.*

## 103 Holding electromagnet (maglock)

*powered & active connectors.* A coil that holds steel firmly while powered and lets go when the power is cut, as in magnetic door locks and robot tool holders

*Not researched yet.*

## 104 Electro-permanent magnet

*powered & active connectors.* A magnet that is switched on or off by a short electric pulse and then holds (or stays released) with no power at all

*Not researched yet.*

## 105 Solenoid latch (solenoid lock, electric strike)

*powered & active connectors.* An electric coil pulls a spring-loaded bolt or catch back when powered, used to unlock doors, boxes and release mechanisms

*Not researched yet.*

## 106 Servo or motorised latch

*powered & active connectors.* A small motor or hobby servo turns a cam, hook or bolt to lock or release, holding its position when power is off

*Not researched yet.*

## 107 Shape-memory-alloy wire latch (nitinol muscle wire)

*powered & active connectors.* A thin nickel-titanium wire that shortens when heated by an electric current, pulling a catch to release a latch silently

*Not researched yet.*

## 108 Electromagnetic brake (power-off, spring-applied)

*powered & active connectors.* Springs clamp a brake on a motor shaft whenever power is off and a coil pulls it free when powered, so robot joints hold if power fails

*Not researched yet.*

## 109 Vacuum (suction) gripper

*powered & active connectors.* A soft cup connected to a vacuum pump or generator that grips smooth objects by suction and lets go when the vacuum is released

*Not researched yet.*

## 110 Granular jamming gripper (coffee-balloon gripper)

*powered & active connectors.* A soft bag of coffee grounds or grains that moulds around an object, then goes rigid and grips it when the air is sucked out

*Not researched yet.*

## 111 Magnet joint (embedded magnets, magnetic catch)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* Pairs of magnets, or a magnet and a steel plate, set into parts so they snap together, align themselves and pull apart by hand

*Not researched yet.*

## 112 Switchable permanent magnet (rotary magnetic base)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* A magnet that is turned on and off by hand with a knob, by rotating one magnet against another, so it holds strongly with no power

*Not researched yet.*

## 113 Aluminium-extrusion connectors (T-slot nuts and brackets)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* The nuts, brackets and hidden fasteners that slide into the grooves of T-slot aluminium extrusion to build 3D-printer, CNC and robot frames without welding

*Not researched yet.*

## 114 Kinematic coupling (balls in V-grooves)

*locating & alignment.* Three balls sitting in three V-grooves so a part drops back into exactly the same position every time; used in tool changers and removable print beds

*Also covers:* Also covers kinematic tool-changer mounts, e.g. on multi-tool 3D printers

*Not researched yet.*

## 115 Self-centring cone and socket (taper locator)

*locating & alignment.* A cone or tapered pin sliding into a matching socket, which guides two parts into line and centres them as they come together

*Not researched yet.*

## 116 Keying (one-way-only fit, poka-yoke)

*locating & alignment.* Uneven pins, notches, tapers or shapes that let a part go together only the right way round, preventing mistakes

*Not researched yet.*

## 117 Constant-force spring (spring-return reel)

*springs & energy storage.* A tightly curled steel strip that pulls with nearly the same force however far it is unrolled, used in tape measures, badge reels and counterweights

*Not researched yet.*

## 118 Ortho-planar spring

*springs & energy storage.* A flat, one-piece spring cut or printed from sheet whose centre moves in and out of the plane; easy to 3D print or laser cut

*Not researched yet.*

## 119 Spring balancer (gravity compensation)

*springs & energy storage.* Springs arranged so they cancel the weight of an arm at any angle, making it feel weightless, as in an anglepoise lamp or a robot arm

*Also covers:* Also covers counterweights as the simplest alternative

*Not researched yet.*

## 120 Printed leaf spring (flat cantilever spring)

*springs & energy storage.* A springy strip or arm of plastic or metal that bends to push back; the most practical kind of spring to 3D print

*Not researched yet.*

## 121 Cam cleat

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* Two spring-loaded toothed cams that let a rope pull through one way and grip it the other way; lift the rope up and out to release

*Not researched yet.*

## 122 Clam cleat (grooved V-cleat)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A block with ridged V-shaped grooves that jams a rope pulled into it and releases when the rope is lifted out; no moving parts

*Not researched yet.*

## 123 Cam buckle (webbing cam buckle)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A buckle with a toothed, spring-loaded cam that grips a strap after you pull it tight; press the lever to release

*Not researched yet.*

## 124 Ladder lock buckle (tension lock, tri-glide)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A slotted buckle that locks a strap by friction when it is loaded and slides when you tilt it; tri-glides are the simpler non-locking version

*Also covers:* Also covers double D-rings and G-hooks

*Not researched yet.*

## 125 Side-release buckle

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A plastic buckle whose two springy arms snap into a housing and release when you squeeze both sides, as on backpacks

*Not researched yet.*

## 126 Cord lock (spring toggle)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A spring-loaded plunger in a small barrel that grips a cord passing through it; squeeze to slide and adjust

*Not researched yet.*

## 127 Magnetic-assisted buckle (magnet guides, catch locks)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A buckle where magnets pull the two halves together and line them up, then a mechanical catch locks them so the magnet doesn't carry the load

*Not researched yet.*

## 128 Belt tensioner (spring-loaded idler)

*tensioning, reels & take-up.* A pulley or roller pushed against a belt or chain by a spring or screw to keep it tight as it stretches

*Not researched yet.*

## 129 Eccentric adjuster (eccentric nut, spacer or bushing)

*tensioning, reels & take-up.* An off-centre spacer or bushing that moves a wheel, pulley or shaft sideways as you turn it, for setting tension or taking up play

*Not researched yet.*

## 130 O-ring seal

*seals & pass-throughs.* A rubber ring squeezed in a groove between two parts to seal against water, air or oil; the most reliable way to make a printed enclosure watertight

*Not researched yet.*

## 131 Gasket (flat, cord-in-groove or cast-in-place)

*seals & pass-throughs.* A soft layer or cord squeezed between a lid and a housing to seal an opening that isn't round, often pressed into a groove by a ridge on the lid

*Not researched yet.*

## 132 Labyrinth seal (baffle seal)

*seals & pass-throughs.* A winding, overlapping path between two parts that keeps out dust and splashes without touching, so it never wears; easy to print into lids and shafts

*Not researched yet.*

## 133 Hose barb (barbed tube fitting)

*seals & pass-throughs.* A spigot with sharp ridges that soft tubing is pushed over and gripped by; for low pressure, with a clip added for more hold

*Not researched yet.*

## 134 Cable gland and strain relief

*seals & pass-throughs.* A threaded fitting whose nut squeezes a rubber insert around a cable, sealing the hole and stopping the cable being pulled out

*Not researched yet.*

## 135 Slip ring and rotary union

*seals & pass-throughs.* A joint with sliding contacts (or seals) that carries power, signals or fluid across something that keeps turning, so cables never wind up, as in robot turrets

*Not researched yet.*

## 136 Cable carrier (drag chain, energy chain)

*seals & pass-throughs.* A chain of hinged hollow links that carries cables and hoses neatly along a moving axis without kinking; one of the most printed functional parts

*Not researched yet.*

## 137 Cyanoacrylate (superglue)

*adhesives & bonding.* A runny or gel glue that sets in seconds by reacting with moisture; strong on tight-fitting plastics, metal and wood but brittle and weak in peel

*Not researched yet.*

## 138 Two-part epoxy

*adhesives & bonding.* A resin and hardener mixed together that set into a strong, gap-filling solid; slow or fast types, for metal, wood, most plastics and composites

*Not researched yet.*

## 139 Solvent welding (solvent cement)

*adhesives & bonding.* A solvent that softens the surfaces of certain plastics so they fuse together as it evaporates, like acetone on ABS or cement on acrylic and PVC

*Not researched yet.*

## 140 UV-curing adhesive (incl. resin used as glue)

*adhesives & bonding.* A liquid that stays wet until hardened in seconds by ultraviolet light, useful for clear parts and for gluing resin prints with the same resin

*Not researched yet.*

## 141 Hot-melt adhesive (glue gun)

*adhesives & bonding.* A plastic stick melted in a glue gun that sticks as it cools; quick and gap-filling but weak and softens with heat

*Not researched yet.*

## 142 Double-sided foam tape (acrylic foam pressure-sensitive adhesive)

*adhesives & bonding.* A tape with sticky foam that grips on contact and stays flexible, mounting parts without drilling; pressure-sensitive adhesive (PSA)

*Not researched yet.*

## 143 Magnetic coupling and magnetic gear

*torque limiting & slip.* Magnets on two parts pass turning force through a gap or a wall without touching, and simply slip if overloaded

*Not researched yet.*

## 144 Tolerance ring (wavy-band press fit)

*torque limiting & slip.* A wavy spring-steel band squeezed between a shaft and a hole that holds by friction, soaks up loose sizes and heat growth, and slips at a set torque

*Not researched yet.*

## 145 Centrifugal clutch

*clutches & couplings.* Weights on a spinning hub fly outwards as speed rises and press against a drum to engage drive automatically, as in go-karts and chainsaws

*Not researched yet.*

## 146 Self-locking wedge (wedge clamp, taper key)

*self-locking & anti-back-drive.* A shallow wedge driven into a gap that stays put by friction, holding parts tightly until tapped back out

*Not researched yet.*

## 147 Inertia-locking reel (seat-belt lock, centrifugal catch)

*self-locking & anti-back-drive.* A reel that pays out freely when pulled slowly but locks instantly when jerked, by a swinging weight or flyweight catching a ratchet

*Not researched yet.*

## 148 Woven self-tightening grip (finger-trap grip)

*self-locking & anti-back-drive.* A braided tube that gets narrower and grips harder the more it is pulled, like a Chinese finger trap or a cable-pulling sock

*Not researched yet.*

## 149 Rope grab and ascender (cam rope clamp)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A toothed cam that lets a clamp slide up a rope but grips hard when loaded downwards, used for climbing and hauling

*Not researched yet.*

## 150 Turnbuckle

*tensioning, reels & take-up.* A threaded sleeve with left- and right-hand threads at its ends, so turning it pulls two rods or cables together to tighten them

*Not researched yet.*

## 151 Capstan and windlass (incl. differential windlass)

*tensioning, reels & take-up.* A drum turned by a crank that winds in rope with a big force; rope wrapped round a drum also holds a large load with a small pull

*Not researched yet.*

## 152 Shock-corded sectional pole

*tensioning, reels & take-up.* Tube sections with elastic cord running through them that pulls each joint home, so a tent pole or wand almost assembles itself

*Not researched yet.*

## 153 Ratchet tie-down strap (ratchet buckle)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A lever with a ratchet that winds webbing onto a slotted spool to tighten a load strap, and releases with a trigger

*Not researched yet.*

## 154 Guy-line slider (friction line tensioner)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A small plate with holes that a cord threads through so it holds by friction under load but slides to adjust when unloaded, as on tent lines

*Not researched yet.*

## 155 Quick-release hooks (pelican hook, three-ring release)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* Hooks and ring stacks that hold a heavy load on a rope or strap but let it go with a small pull on a pin, lever or cord

*Also covers:* Also covers the snap shackle

*Not researched yet.*

## 156 Spring-gate hook (carabiner, snap hook, lobster clasp)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* A hook with a spring-loaded gate that snaps shut behind a ring or rope, sometimes with a screw or twist lock on the gate

*Not researched yet.*

## 157 Hook-and-loop fastener

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* Two fabric strips, one with tiny hooks and one with loops, that stick together when pressed and peel apart

*Not researched yet.*

## 158 Zipper (slide fastener)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* Two rows of teeth that a slider meshes together or separates as it moves, closing a long opening in one pull

*Not researched yet.*

## 159 Cam latch (quarter-turn cam lock)

*locks, latches & catches.* A knob or key turns a flat arm (cam) behind a panel so it hooks over the frame and holds a door or lid shut

*Not researched yet.*

## 160 Mechanical interlock

*locks, latches & catches.* A blocking part that stops one action while another is happening, such as a lid that can't open while a machine runs

*Not researched yet.*

## 161 Pin-tumbler lock (and other tumbler locks)

*locks, latches & catches.* A key lifts spring-loaded pins or levers to exactly the right heights so a plug can turn; the everyday door lock

*Not researched yet.*

## 162 Combination lock (wheel pack)

*locks, latches & catches.* Several wheels with notches that line up only when each is turned to the right number, letting a bar or shackle move

*Not researched yet.*

## 163 Rotary latch (claw latch)

*locks, latches & catches.* A rotating jaw that swings shut around a striker bar and is held by a catch, as in car doors and hatches; releases when a lever or cable trips the catch

*Not researched yet.*

## 164 Folding-blade lock (liner lock, lockback, slip joint)

*locks, latches & catches.* A pivot that locks a folding blade or arm open when a spring bar or rocker drops behind it; a slip joint only holds it with a detent

*Not researched yet.*

## 165 Tamper-evident breakaway bridge (frangible link)

*locks, latches & catches.* Thin plastic bridges that must snap the first time a cap or lid is opened, showing that it has been opened

*Not researched yet.*

## 166 Quick couplers for tubes and hoses (push-to-connect, sleeve lock)

*quick-release & tool-free.* Fittings that grip a tube when it is pushed in and release when a collar is pressed or a sleeve is pulled back

*Not researched yet.*

## 167 DIN-rail clip (top-hat rail snap mount)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A spring clip that hooks over one edge of a standard metal rail and snaps onto the other, so modules click on and off

*Not researched yet.*

## 168 Indexable clamping lever (ratchet handle)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A clamping handle on a toothed spline that you lift to swing round without turning the screw, then let spring back to tighten again, for tight spaces

*Not researched yet.*

## 169 Spring bar (spring-loaded telescoping pin)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A tube with spring-loaded ends that pop into holes to hold a strap between two lugs, as on watch straps; quick-release versions have a small lever

*Not researched yet.*

## 170 Band flange clamp (V-band clamp, tri-clamp)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A hinged band with a V-shaped groove that wedges two flanged ends together as it is tightened, for quick-disconnect pipes, ducts and brewing kit

*Not researched yet.*

## 171 Temporary sheet fastener (Cleco-type spring pin)

*quick-release & tool-free.* A spring-loaded pin fitted from one side with pliers that clamps sheets in line through a hole while they are drilled or riveted, then comes out again

*Not researched yet.*

## 172 Press stud (snap fastener, popper)

*snap & clip fits.* A small metal or plastic stud that snaps into a socket with a springy ring, as on clothing and bags

*Not researched yet.*

## 173 Spring-steel push-on fasteners (push nut, speed nut, U-nut)

*snap & clip fits.* Stamped spring-steel clips whose prongs bite onto a plain shaft or screw when pushed on, fast to fit and resistant to shaking loose

*Not researched yet.*

## 174 Lamina emergent torsional joint (LET joint)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* A pattern of cuts in flat sheet that lets part of it twist out of the plane like a hinge, for laser-cut or printed flat mechanisms

*Not researched yet.*

## 175 Rolling-contact joint (CORE, Jacob's-ladder hinge)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* Two curved surfaces held together by straps or bands so they roll on each other instead of rubbing, giving a low-friction hinge

*Not researched yet.*

## 176 Mechanical metamaterial mechanism (shear-cell hinge arrays)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* A block made of a repeating grid of tiny cells that bend in a planned way, so the whole block works like a mechanism

*Not researched yet.*

## 177 Tape-spring hinge (measuring-tape fold)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* A curved metal or plastic strip that folds with a sharp kink and snaps back straight and stiff, like a tape measure, used in deployable booms

*Not researched yet.*

## 178 Origami mechanisms (waterbomb, Kresling, thick-panel origami)

*compliant mechanisms & flexures.* Fold patterns used as joints so flat sheets or panels fold up, expand or twist in a planned way

*Not researched yet.*

## 179 Press-fit and expanding inserts (tap-in insert)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* Threaded inserts that are pushed or tapped into a hole and held by barbs, or expand as the screw goes in, as an alternative to heat-set inserts

*Not researched yet.*

## 180 Gimbal

*hinges & pivots.* Rings pivoted at right angles to each other so the object in the middle can tilt freely in two or three directions, as in camera stabilisers

*Not researched yet.*

## 181 Clip-on concealed hinge (cup hinge)

*hinges & pivots.* A hidden cabinet hinge that sits in a round pocket in the door and clips on and off its mounting plate without tools, with adjusting screws

*Not researched yet.*

## 182 Invisible barrel hinge (Soss-type)

*hinges & pivots.* A hinge made of folding links that hides completely inside the edges of the door and frame when closed

*Not researched yet.*

## 183 Virtual-pivot linkage (remote centre of motion)

*hinges & pivots.* A linkage that makes a part rotate about a point in empty space where there is no actual pin, as in some robot wrists and folding mechanisms

*Not researched yet.*

## 184 Self-closing hinges (spring hinge, rising butt hinge)

*hinges & pivots.* Hinges that swing a door or lid shut by themselves, using a spring or a sloping knuckle that lifts the door as it opens

*Not researched yet.*

## 185 Lid and flap stays (folding stay, friction stay)

*hinges & pivots.* Arms that hold a lid or flap open: a folding stay locks just past straight, a sliding stay holds by adjustable friction

*Not researched yet.*

## 186 Drawer slide (telescopic ball-bearing slide)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* Nested metal channels with ball bearings that let a drawer or robot lift extend smoothly, sometimes with a lock-out or soft close

*Not researched yet.*

## 187 Ball screw

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* A screw and nut with balls rolling between them, turning rotation into straight-line motion with very low friction; it can be back-driven

*Not researched yet.*

## 188 Differential screw (fine adjustment screw)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* One screw with two slightly different thread pitches, so each turn moves the part by only the small difference, for very fine adjustment

*Not researched yet.*

## 189 Rigid chain actuator (zip chain, push chain)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* A chain whose links lock into a stiff column as they come out of a housing, so it can push as well as pull and packs away small

*Not researched yet.*

## 190 Spline shaft (sliding spline)

*clutches & couplings.* A shaft with lengthwise ridges fitting a matching hub, passing turning force while letting the hub slide along the shaft

*Also covers:* Also covers hex shafts and LEGO-style cross axles

*Not researched yet.*

## 191 Constant-velocity joint (CV joint)

*clutches & couplings.* A joint that drives a shaft at an angle without the speed wobble of a simple universal joint, using balls in curved tracks

*Not researched yet.*

## 192 Friction clutch (plate or cone clutch)

*clutches & couplings.* Friction surfaces pressed together to connect drive and pulled apart to disconnect it, able to slip gently while engaging

*Not researched yet.*

## 193 Keyless locking bush (taper-lock bush, shaft locking device)

*clutches & couplings.* Tapered rings squeezed by screws that grip a smooth shaft and hub by friction, fixing a pulley or gear without keys or flats

*Not researched yet.*

## 194 Schmidt coupling (offset coupling)

*clutches & couplings.* Three discs joined by links that pass rotation between two parallel shafts which are well out of line, at constant speed

*Not researched yet.*

## 195 Flexible drive shaft (rotating cable)

*clutches & couplings.* Layers of wound wire in a sleeve that carry rotation round bends, as in rotary-tool extensions and speedometer cables

*Not researched yet.*

## 196 Quick-return mechanism (Whitworth, crank and slotted lever)

*motion conversion.* A crank driving a slotted lever so the working stroke is slow and the return stroke is fast, as in shaping machines

*Not researched yet.*

## 197 Straight-line linkages (Watt, Chebyshev, Peaucellier)

*motion conversion.* Linkages of pivoted bars arranged so one point moves in a straight or nearly straight line without any slide

*Also covers:* Also covers the Tusi couple (a rolling-circle straight-line mechanism)

*Not researched yet.*

## 198 Escapement (anchor or verge escapement)

*motion conversion.* A rocking catch that lets a spring- or weight-driven wheel advance one tooth at a time in a steady rhythm, as in clocks

*Not researched yet.*

## 199 Bell crank (angle lever)

*motion conversion.* An L-shaped lever on a pivot that turns a push or pull round a corner, and can change its size, as in RC and aircraft controls

*Not researched yet.*

## 200 Swashplate and wobble plate

*motion conversion.* A tilted disc on a shaft that turns rotation into back-and-forth motion of rods around it, or the reverse; also tilts helicopter blades

*Not researched yet.*

## 201 Anti-backlash gear (split scissor gear)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A gear made of two halves sprung against each other so its teeth fill the gaps of the mating gear, removing play

*Not researched yet.*

## 202 Chain and sprocket drive

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A roller chain running on toothed wheels (sprockets) to pass strong, non-slipping rotation between shafts some distance apart

*Not researched yet.*

## 203 Non-circular gears

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* Oval or oddly shaped gears that mesh while turning, so the output speeds up and slows down during each turn

*Not researched yet.*

## 204 Lantern gear (pin gear)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A gear made of pins between two discs, easy to make from dowels or laser-cut sheet, used in mills and simple machines

*Not researched yet.*

## 205 Friction drives and CVTs (friction wheel, variator)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* A wheel or roller pressed against a disc, cone or another wheel that drives it by friction with no teeth, so it has no backlash, slips when overloaded and, on cones or discs, can change ratio smoothly

*Not researched yet.*

## 206 Pulley system (block and tackle, differential hoist)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* Ropes running over several pulleys so a small pull lifts a big load; a differential chain hoist also holds the load by itself

*Not researched yet.*

## 207 Electromagnetic clutch

*powered & active connectors.* A coil that pulls two plates together to connect a drive when powered and lets them separate when switched off

*Not researched yet.*

## 208 Thermal actuators (bimetal strip, wax motor)

*powered & active connectors.* Parts that move when heated, a strip of two bonded metals that bends or a capsule of wax that expands, used to trip or open a latch

*Not researched yet.*

## 209 Burn-wire and fusible-link release (one-shot thermal release)

*powered & active connectors.* A cord or low-melting link that holds something until an electric current or heat melts or cuts it, as in satellite and balloon releases

*Not researched yet.*

## 210 Compression fitting (ferrule fitting)

*seals & pass-throughs.* A nut that squeezes a ring (ferrule or olive) onto a tube to grip and seal it, undone and remade with spanners

*Not researched yet.*

## 211 Friction brake (band, drum or disc brake)

*brakes, dampers & speed control.* Friction pads or a band pressed on a turning drum or disc to slow or hold it; a band brake grips harder the further it wraps round

*Not researched yet.*

## 212 Eddy-current brake (magnetic brake)

*brakes, dampers & speed control.* A magnet near a moving metal plate that drags on it without touching, more strongly the faster it moves; smooth but can't hold anything still

*Not researched yet.*

## 213 Speed governor (fly fan, centrifugal governor)

*brakes, dampers & speed control.* Air vanes or flyweights that keep a spring- or weight-driven mechanism running at a steady speed, as in music boxes and clock chimes

*Not researched yet.*

## 214 Dampers (rotary and linear soft-close dampers)

*brakes, dampers & speed control.* Parts filled with oil or air that slow a movement down, so lids, drawers and doors close gently instead of slamming

*Not researched yet.*

## 215 Elastomer isolation mount (rubber bobbin mount, isolator grommet)

*brakes, dampers & speed control.* Rubber blocks, grommets or bobbins between a part and its frame that soak up vibration and noise, as under 3D-printer motors and fans

*Not researched yet.*

## 216 Suction cup

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* A soft rubber cup pressed onto a smooth surface so air pressure holds it on; some have a lever to increase the suction

*Not researched yet.*

## 217 Taper fit (Morse taper)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* A slightly tapered shaft pushed into a matching tapered hole that locks by friction and is knocked apart with a wedge, as in drill chucks

*Also covers:* Also covers the Luer taper and Luer lock (syringe and tubing fittings)

*Not researched yet.*

## 218 Press fit (interference fit)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* A part pushed into a hole that is slightly too small for it, so it is held by the squeeze alone (bearings, pins, printed parts)

*Not researched yet.*

## 219 Collet (and keyless chuck)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* A slotted sleeve squeezed by a nut or taper so it closes evenly around a round shaft or tool, gripping it centred

*Not researched yet.*

## 220 Clamp-on tube fittings (slip-on structural fittings)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* Tees, elbows and crosses that slide onto round tube and lock with set screws, building frames and rails that can be taken apart

*Not researched yet.*

## 221 Internal tube anchors (star nut, expansion plug)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* Parts pushed into the end of a tube that grip the inside with barbs, a squeezed rubber plug or a wedge, giving a thread or a clamp point

*Not researched yet.*

## 222 Blind threaded fixings (rivet nut, well nut)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* Threaded inserts fitted from one side only that swell behind thin sheet or a tube wall; a rubber well nut also seals and damps vibration

*Not researched yet.*

## 223 PVA wood glue (white and yellow glue)

*adhesives & bonding.* A water-based glue that dries by soaking into porous materials like wood, paper and cork; strong on wood, with water-resistant grades

*Not researched yet.*

## 224 Contact adhesive (contact cement)

*adhesives & bonding.* A rubbery glue spread on both surfaces, left to dry until tacky, then pressed together to bond instantly; good for sheets, rubber, fabric and leather

*Not researched yet.*

## 225 Polyurethane glue (foaming moisture-cure glue)

*adhesives & bonding.* A glue that cures by reacting with moisture and foams as it sets, filling gaps; bonds wood, stone, metal, foam and some plastics

*Not researched yet.*

## 226 Structural acrylic and low-surface-energy plastic adhesives (for PE and PP)

*adhesives & bonding.* Two-part acrylic glues, and primers used with superglue, that bond tough slippery plastics like polyethylene and polypropylene which most glues can't

*Not researched yet.*

## 227 Silicone and MS-polymer adhesive sealants

*adhesives & bonding.* Rubbery glues that cure from moisture in the air into a flexible, waterproof bond that also seals gaps

*Not researched yet.*

## 228 Elastomeric solvent glue (flexible craft glue)

*adhesives & bonding.* Thick, clear, rubbery solvent-based glues that stay flexible, for bonding dissimilar materials, fabric, shoes and jewellery

*Not researched yet.*

## 229 Anaerobic adhesive (threadlocker, retaining compound)

*adhesives & bonding.* A liquid that hardens only when shut off from air between close-fitting metal parts, used to stop screws loosening or to fix bearings on shafts

*Not researched yet.*

## 230 Stretch-release adhesive strips

*adhesives & bonding.* Sticky strips that hold firmly but come away cleanly when you pull the tab slowly along the wall, leaving no residue

*Not researched yet.*

## 231 Hide glue (animal glue)

*adhesives & bonding.* A traditional glue made from animal collagen that can be undone later with heat and moisture, used in instrument making and furniture repair

*Not researched yet.*

## 232 Epoxy putty (kneadable two-part putty)

*adhesives & bonding.* Two doughs kneaded together that set hard in place, for filling gaps, repairing and building up shapes that can then be drilled and tapped

*Not researched yet.*

## 233 Elastic averaging (many-feature alignment)

*locating & alignment.* Many small springy features (ribs, bumps, teeth) that each flex a little so their errors average out, locating parts accurately and repeatably

*Not researched yet.*

## 234 Locating pins and hole-and-slot

*locating & alignment.* One pin in a round hole and one in a slot, so parts line up in exactly one position without jamming when sizes vary a little

*Not researched yet.*

## 235 Exact-constraint mounting (3-2-1 locating)

*locating & alignment.* Holding a part with exactly as many contact points as it needs (three on a face, two on an edge, one on an end), so it sits the same way every time without strain

*Not researched yet.*

## 236 Wave and disc springs (compact axial springs)

*springs & energy storage.* Wavy flat rings or dished washers that give a strong spring force in a very short height, used to preload bearings and take up play

*Not researched yet.*

## 237 Gas spring (incl. lockable gas spring)

*springs & energy storage.* A sealed cylinder of pressurised gas whose rod pushes out with a steady force, holding up lids and hatches; lockable types hold at any length

*Not researched yet.*

## 238 Spiral power spring (clock spring)

*springs & energy storage.* A flat strip coiled into a spiral that stores energy when wound and releases it as it unwinds, as in clockwork and retracting cords

*Not researched yet.*

## 239 Tab-and-slot joint (self-fixturing sheet joint)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Tabs on one flat part fit through slots in another so the parts hold each other in place, for laser-cut, CNC and sheet-metal assemblies

*Not researched yet.*

## 240 T-slot joint (captive nut and bolt joint)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A T-shaped cut-out in the edge of a sheet holds a square nut so a bolt through the other part can clamp them together; common in laser-cut boxes

*Not researched yet.*

## 241 Snap-lock sheet joint (barbed or spring finger)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Sheet tabs with barbs or springy fingers that click into slots and stay put with no glue or screws

*Not researched yet.*

## 242 Cross-halving slot joint (egg-crate joint)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Two flat parts each slotted halfway so they slide together into a cross, used for grids, dividers and slot-together furniture

*Not researched yet.*

## 243 Finger joint (box joint)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Interlocking rectangular fingers along two edges that join at a corner, as in laser-cut boxes and wooden drawers

*Not researched yet.*

## 244 Kerf bending (laser-cut living hinge)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A pattern of cuts that lets stiff sheet like plywood or acrylic bend around curves or fold like a hinge

*Not researched yet.*

## 245 Dovetail joint (through and half-blind)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Flared tails and pins that interlock so the joint can't be pulled apart in one direction; the classic drawer corner

*Not researched yet.*

## 246 Sliding dovetail

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A flared tongue that slides along a matching flared groove, locking against pull-out while still sliding lengthwise

*Not researched yet.*

## 247 Mortise and tenon (incl. wedged, drawbored and tusk tenons)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A tongue (tenon) on one part fits a hole (mortise) in the other; wedges, offset pegs or a removable tusk wedge lock it

*Not researched yet.*

## 248 Butterfly key (bow-tie key)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A bow-tie-shaped insert set into both parts across a joint or crack so they can't pull apart; also used as a CNC sheet connector

*Not researched yet.*

## 249 Japanese interlocking joints (splices and three-way corners)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Traditional wood joints like the gooseneck splice and the three-way corner that lock together by shape alone, often with wedges or pegs

*Not researched yet.*

## 250 Knock-down cam-lock and cross-dowel connectors

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Flat-pack fittings where a turning cam grabs a bolt head, or a barrel nut takes a bolt through the other panel, so furniture can be taken apart

*Not researched yet.*

## 251 Wedge dowel (click-in grooved dowel)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A ribbed dowel that clicks into a groove and wedges the panels tightly together, with no screws or tools

*Not researched yet.*

## 252 Lever-cam panel connector (self-clamping click connector)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A hidden fitting set into a slot that is locked with a turn of a key or clicks shut by itself, pulling panels together; releasable

*Not researched yet.*

## 253 Folded seam (hem, lock seam, Pittsburgh lock)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Sheet-metal edges folded over each other and pressed flat so they lock together without welding or fasteners

*Not researched yet.*

## 254 Bent or twisted tab through a slot (lanced tab)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A tab on one sheet-metal part goes through a slot in the other and is bent over or twisted to lock them together

*Not researched yet.*

## 255 Clinching (press joining without rivets)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A punch and die press two metal sheets into a button shape that locks them together, with no rivet, screw or heat

*Not researched yet.*

## 256 Self-clinching fastener (press-in nut or stud)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A nut, stud or standoff pressed into a hole in thin sheet metal so the metal flows into its grooves and holds it for good

*Not researched yet.*

## 257 Ordinary screw joint (bolt and nut, machine screw)

*clamping, magnetic & friction connections.* A bolt through the parts into a nut or tapped thread, clamping them together; the baseline everything else is compared with

*Not researched yet.*

## 258 Self-tapping screw into plastic (thread-forming screw)

*print-in-place & 3D-printing joints.* A screw that cuts or forms its own thread in a plain hole in a printed or moulded part; simple but wears out after a few uses

*Not researched yet.*

## 259 Retaining ring (circlip, E-clip)

*snap & clip fits.* A springy metal ring that snaps into a groove on a shaft or in a hole to stop parts sliding off

*Not researched yet.*

## 260 Thread-locking hardware (nylon-insert nut, lock washer, split pin)

*self-locking & anti-back-drive.* Nuts, washers and pins that stop threaded joints shaking loose by friction, springiness or a physical block

*Not researched yet.*

## 261 Tool-free fasteners (thumbscrew, wing nut, hitch pin and R-clip)

*quick-release & tool-free.* Ordinary fasteners shaped to be done up by hand, as a simple quick-release baseline

*Not researched yet.*

## 262 Basic latches (spring latch, slide bolt, hook and eye)

*locks, latches & catches.* The everyday door latch with a sloped spring bolt, a sliding bolt, and a hook in an eye; the latch baseline

*Not researched yet.*

## 263 Knuckle hinge (butt hinge)

*hinges & pivots.* Two leaves joined by a pin through interlocking knuckles; the ordinary door hinge

*Not researched yet.*

## 264 Plain pivot and rolling bearing (pin in hole, shoulder bolt, ball bearing)

*hinges & pivots.* A shaft or pin turning in a hole, or in a ball bearing, as the basic way to make something rotate

*Not researched yet.*

## 265 Linear guides (bushings on rods, wheels or carriages on rails)

*sliding, telescoping & linear motion.* The standard ways to make something slide in a straight line, from plastic bushings on steel rods to wheels and carriages on rails

*Not researched yet.*

## 266 Shaft-to-hub joints and rigid couplings (key, set screw, D-flat, clamp hub)

*clutches & couplings.* The ordinary ways to fix a gear, pulley or wheel to a shaft, or join two shafts rigidly end to end

*Also covers:* Also covers shaft collars

*Not researched yet.*

## 267 Spur gear pair and gear train (simple, compound, idler)

*gear trains, gearboxes & transmissions.* Straight-toothed gears on parallel shafts, alone or in chains, as the baseline way to change speed and turning force

*Not researched yet.*

## 268 Coil spring (compression, extension, torsion)

*springs & energy storage.* Wire wound into a coil that pushes, pulls or twists back; the baseline spring

*Not researched yet.*

## 269 Basic strap and garment closures (prong buckle, button, toggle and loop)

*cord, strap, webbing & textile hardware.* The everyday belt buckle with a prong, buttons and toggles in loops; the strap and textile baseline

*Not researched yet.*

## 270 Glued joint design (butt, lap and scarf joints; peel vs shear)

*adhesives & bonding.* How the shape of a glued joint decides its strength, since most glues are strong in shear and weak when peeled or pulled straight apart

*Not researched yet.*

## 271 Butt and lap wood joints (screwed, pocket-hole, half-lap)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Simple wood joints where parts meet end to face or overlap, held by screws, glue or angled pocket screws; the joinery baseline

*Not researched yet.*

## 272 Dowel and biscuit joints

*interlocking joints & joinery.* Round wooden pins or flat oval wafers glued into matching holes or slots in both parts to align and strengthen a joint

*Not researched yet.*

## 273 Tongue-and-groove joint (incl. sliding-lid grooves)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A ridge on one edge fitting a groove in the other, joining boards edge to edge or letting a lid slide into a box

*Not researched yet.*

## 274 Blind rivet (pop rivet)

*interlocking joints & joinery.* A hollow rivet set from one side with a hand tool that pulls a mandrel to swell the far end; the sheet-metal baseline

*Not researched yet.*
